# `#519` sgirandomcolour — PASS 2 ON THE REAL DIFF: the colour-type PUT default wrote four host-PRNG bytes into guest memory; it now declines the store once-announced

## READ THIS FIRST — PLEASE DO NOT SIMPLY RATIFY

A recent panel had seven of nine seats confirm a premise this brief's author stated
wrongly.  **Check the premises, not just the derivations.**  Where I state something as
fact, ask whether it is true.  A seat that re-derives a premise from something this brief
did NOT give it is worth more than a seat that agrees.

copies of GXemul under the project root (`./src/` and two under
`_archive/audit-results-*/builds/`).  They are NOT this fork.  If your line numbers
disagree with mine by hundreds you are reading one of them — say so rather than

reachable addresses — ENUMERATE it rather than reasoning about it.**  Walk the values.
A derivation that skips one masking line reads exactly like a correct one.

**And ENUMERATE THE RIGHT DOMAIN — a domain is itself a premise.**  Ask what the PRODUCER of
these values can actually emit before you walk them.  Walking every member of a set you have
not audited is not enumeration; it is a derivation with more steps, and it reads exactly like

## §C — SETTLED PREMISES, RE-INLINED (say which you cannot settle from this packet)
S1. THE DEFECT. `horrible_getputpixel()` in `dev_sgi_re.c`: the PUT branch switches on
    `color_mode = mode & DE_MODE_TYPE_MASK` (0xf0). Four cases (CI 0x00, RGB 0x10, RGBA 0x20, ABGR 0x30 --
    `crmfbreg.h:328-331`); the `default:` arm assigned `buf[0..3] = random()`, called an UNLATCHED per-pixel
    `fatal()` (which RETURNS: `debugmsg.c:384`, no exit), and fell through to the common
    `cpu->memory_rw(... buf, bufdepth, MEM_WRITE ...)`. Twelve of sixteen nibbles reach it, `DE_MODE_TYPE_YCRCB`
    (0xf0, documented) among them.
S2. WITNESS (rung 3, agy seat, unmodified `-E sgi -e ip32`, DE rectangle PUT through LIN_A at a physical tile,
    destination pre-painted 0x11223344, fg 0xaabbccdd): on HEAD `SGICOLOUR_RESULT=2/5` -- the YCRCB PUT read
    0x56e8857a (run 1) / 0x38abc4e1 (run 2), the reserved-type 0x50 PUT likewise, neither the paint nor any
    packing of the colour, different across runs because `main.c` seeds `random()` per process; attribution
    text `[ put: color mode = 0xf0 ]` present. On the fix, 5/5 with the same script; with the nine detector
    rows below, 14/14 on the fix and 6/14 on HEAD (`regress/artifacts/sgirandomcolour/witness_20260910/`,
    `fix_20260910/`).
S3. DESIGN (pass-1 panel 2026-09-08, five seats; codex DESIGN2 reading; flagship ruling 2026-09-09): option-set
    member 3, candidate (b) -- SKIP THE STORE for an unsupported type (a one-local `ok` guard around the single
    store, never an early `return`: the common tail is that store), delete the random() fills and the per-pixel
    fatal, announce ONCE per device instance on an INDEPENDENT latch bit. Rejected: (a) zero-fill-and-store
    (publishes a colour the guest never asked for). The GET default arm is a SEPARATE row (`sgirebufuninit`)
    and is untouched. The old literal `[ put: color mode = 0x%x ]` is kept as the announce's prefix because the
    witness attributes on it.
S4. THE LATCH: `SGIRE_W_BADCOLOR = 1 << 12`, added as the LAST enumerator with the enum's own "next free bit"
    sentence moved to it and the previous holder's copy demoted (the convention every entry in that enum
    records). `SGIRE_W_BADMODE` (1 << 10) is the same function's TLB/mode-switch decline (`#504`) -- a different
    event with a different text.
S5. PRE-TABLE CHECK written before any table (`killtable_20260910/PRETABLE.out`, inlined at the end).

## THE DETECTOR ROWS APPENDED TO regress/sgi_re_colourdefault_probe.py (verbatim; the five witness rows above them are unchanged)
```python
# ================================================================ #519 DETECTOR ROWS
#  The five rows above are the WITNESS (2/5 on HEAD 1ce8296 -- both unsupported-type PUTs landed host
#  PRNG bytes -- and 5/5 on the fix with the same script).  The rows below DEFEND the fix.
#
#  CONTRACT (clause (e)).  Designed to catch: (b) the verbatim arm reinstated (random fills + an
#  unlatched fatal, the store still taken -- `ycrcb put', `reserved put', `announce text'); (d) the
#  `ok' guard REMOVED (the store taken with an unassigned buf -- `ycrcb put'); the decline turned
#  into a ZERO FILL and a store (`ycrcb put' reads 0); `ok = 0' MOVED INSIDE THE LATCH so only the
#  first declined PUT skips its store (`two declined'); the latch REMOVED (`once' reads 2); the latch
#  SHARED with SGIRE_W_BADMODE (`latch indep' reads 0 after a bad TLB mode fired that bit); the
#  announce SILENCED (`announce text' reads 0); a supported type dropped from the switch (its accept
#  row); the accept side announcing (`live silent').  NOT rowed, disclosed: the GET default arm (a
#  separate row, sgirebufuninit); 16-bit depth (the PUT arms assign four bytes and a 16-bit store
#  takes two -- the same row's question); `x`/`y` in the announce (format only); per-instance latching
#  (one RE instance exists); the YCRCB/reserved values other than 0xf0/0x50 (twelve nibbles reach the
#  arm; two are driven, and the arm does not read the nibble's value).
#
#  The destination word is read big-endian (ip32): byte 0 of the tile is the word's top byte.

ANN = "unimplemented colour type in horrible_getputpixel"     # the post-fix announce, distinctive part
DECL = "store declined (once)"
MODE_CI8  = 0x1000            # LIN_A | BUFDEPTH_8  | TYPE_CI   -- one byte stored
MODE_RGB  = 0x1210            # LIN_A | BUFDEPTH_32 | TYPE_RGB
MODE_ABGR = 0x1230            # LIN_A | BUFDEPTH_32 | TYPE_ABGR
MODE_LINB_RGBA = 0x1620       # LIN_B | BUFDEPTH_32 | TYPE_RGBA -- the TLB/mode switch's own default (BADMODE)

#  ---- accept rows: the other three supported types land their packing (de-escalation) -----------
A = Addr(CODE)
t_ci, alive_ci = session(setup_put(A, MODE_CI8) + load(A, DST_KSEG1) + ["print v0"])
row("ci8 put", printed(t_ci, "v0") if alive_ci else "died", "0xdd223344",
    "CI at 8 bpp stores ONE byte (fg & 0xff) over the paint's top byte")
A = Addr(CODE)
t_rgb, alive_rgb = session(setup_put(A, MODE_RGB) + load(A, DST_KSEG1) + ["print v0"])
row("rgb put", printed(t_rgb, "v0") if alive_rgb else "died", "0xaabbcc00",
    "RGB stores [R,G,B,pad]: three channels and a zero pad (#505's authoritative arm)")
A = Addr(CODE)
t_abgr, alive_abgr = session(setup_put(A, MODE_ABGR) + load(A, DST_KSEG1) + ["print v0"])
row("abgr put", printed(t_abgr, "v0") if alive_abgr else "died", "0xddccbbaa",
    "ABGR stores the register word byte-reversed")
row("live silent", t_live.count(ANN) if alive_live else "died", 0,
    "the RGBA accept session carries NO colour-type announce (the accept side stays silent)")

#  ---- the announce: present once, with the post-fix text, in the witness's ycrcb session --------
row("announce text", t_ycrcb1.count(DECL) if alive_ycrcb1 else "died", 1,
    "the declined PUT announces with the post-fix text ONCE (a reinstated arm or a silenced decline reads 0)")

#  ---- two declined PUTs in ONE process: the second also skips its store, and one announce -------
A = Addr(CODE)
t_two, alive_two = session(setup_put(A, MODE_YCRCB) + setup_put(A, MODE_RESERVED)
                           + load(A, DST_KSEG1) + ["print v0"])
row("two declined", printed(t_two, "v0") if alive_two else "died", "0x%08x" % DST_PAINT,
    "YCRCB then RESERVED in one process: the SECOND declined PUT leaves the paint too (`ok = 0' inside the latch would store)")
row("once", t_two.count(ANN) if alive_two else "died", 1,
    "two declined PUTs announce EXACTLY ONCE -- the (once) latch")

#  ---- the latch bit is its own: a BADMODE decline first, then a colour decline still announces --
A = Addr(CODE)
t_ind, alive_ind = session(setup_put(A, MODE_LINB_RGBA) + setup_put(A, MODE_YCRCB)
                           + load(A, DST_KSEG1) + ["print v0"])
row("latch indep", t_ind.count(ANN) if alive_ind else "died", 1,
    "LIN_B fires SGIRE_W_BADMODE first; the colour decline still announces once (a shared bit reads 0)")
row("indep paint", printed(t_ind, "v0") if alive_ind else "died", "0x%08x" % DST_PAINT,
    "and neither declined PUT stored (LIN_B returns before the store; YCRCB skips it)")

```

## THE PRE-TABLE CHECK (verbatim)
```
#519 sgirandomcolour -- PRE-TABLE CHECK, written 2026-09-10 ~04:2x BEFORE table 1 runs (mtime is the proof).
Instrument: regress/sgi_re_colourdefault_probe.py -- 5 witness rows (agy, 2/5 on HEAD 1ce8296, 5/5 on the
fix) + 9 detector rows appended by the main loop.  Diff under review: src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c
(scratchpad/patch_sgirc.py, 58 changed lines): SGIRE_W_BADCOLOR = 1 << 12 as the last enumerator, an `ok'
local in the PUT branch, the default arm declining (ok = 0, once-latched announce keeping the old
`[ put: color mode = 0x%x ]' literal as its prefix), the store guarded by `if (ok)'.  Product code
otherwise untouched; the GET default arm is NOT in scope (sgirebufuninit).

1. SIBLING PARITY
   The PUT default arm's sibling is the GET default arm in the same function (reads raw, announces
   per pixel, unlatched) -- NOT touched here; its row is sgirebufuninit and it is DISCLOSED in the
   contract.  The TLB/mode switch's default (SGIRE_W_BADMODE, #504) is the nearest de-fatalised
   sibling: latched, returns before any store.  Channels there: announce once, `*color' untouched.
   Channels here: announce once (its OWN bit -- `latch indep' rows the independence), destination
   untouched (`ycrcb put', `reserved put', `two declined', `indep paint').  ASYMMETRY: #504's arm
   RETURNS; this one falls through to the common tail with the store skipped -- deliberate (the
   sketch rule: the tail's memory_rw is the only thing after the switch, and it is what the guard
   skips), and the `two declined' row is what pins "skipped" rather than "returned early" for the
   second PUT.
2. CLAIM -> ROW
   "the STORE IS SKIPPED, so the destination keeps what the guest had"     -> ycrcb put, reserved put,
                                                                              two declined, indep paint
   "not a zero fill"                                                        -> ycrcb put (0 would red)
   "announce is latched on its own bit"                                     -> once, latch indep
   "the old literal is kept as the prefix: the witness attributes on it"   -> ycrcb attrib (unchanged),
                                                                              announce text
   "A declined PUT never reads buf"                                          -> no row: not observable
                                                                              (no store); SENTENCE.
   "Twelve of the sixteen type nibbles reach here"                          -> two driven (0xf0, 0x50);
                                                                              the arm does not read the
                                                                              value; SENTENCE (contract).
   "main.c seeds random() per process"                                      -> the witness's determinism
                                                                              row measured it; no fix row.
3. GUARD FIELD vs BODY FIELD
   Guard: color_mode (a local from `mode').  Body: `ok', d->warned.  The shared field d->warned now
   has thirteen bits; the disagreeing state the rule asks for is another site's bit set first --
   built in-session by `latch indep' (SGIRE_W_BADMODE fired by a LIN_B PUT, then the colour decline).
   `ok' is a local written only by the default arm: its disagreeing state is "second declined PUT in
   the same process with the latch already set", built by `two declined'.
4. CHANNEL -- per row, the mutant it kills and the observable that differs
   ycrcb put / reserved put   (b) reinstatement (random fills + store); M_noguard (`if (ok)' removed:
                              stores an unassigned buf); M_zerofill (buf = 0, ok = 1)     -> word != paint
   two declined               M_okinlatch (`ok = 0' moved inside the latch condition)      -> word != paint
   once                       M_nolatch (latch guard removed)                              -> count 2
   latch indep                M_sharedbit (SGIRE_W_BADMODE used instead of BADCOLOR)       -> count 0
   indep paint                M_sharedbit does not vary this; (b) does (LIN_B returns; YCRCB stores) -> word
   announce text              (b) (old text only) ; M_silent (announce removed)             -> count 0
   live silent                M_declineall (ok = 0 for every type) ; M_announceall            -> count 1 / word
   ci8 / rgb / abgr put       M_dropcase_X (the case removed -> falls to default -> paint)  -> word
   VACUOUS-BY-CONSTRUCTION CHECK: `indep paint' is fixed under M_sharedbit (both PUTs skip their
   store either way) -- it is a CONTROL for `latch indep', named as such; every other row's channel
   varies under its named mutant.
5. THE (d) ENUMERATION FOR TABLE 1 (bounded to the diff's hunks):
   dominating predicates: `put' (unchanged), `switch (color_mode)' (each supported type its own
   case; the default is one cell), `if (ok)' (introduced), the latch test (introduced).  Cells:
   {default declines / stores} x {latch kept / removed / shared} x {store guard kept / removed /
   inside-latch} x {supported types kept / one dropped}.  Planned mutants, one edit each:
   M1_reinstate (b)   M2_noguard   M3_zerofill   M4_okinlatch   M5_nolatch   M6_sharedbit
   M7_silent          M8_declineall   M9_dropcase_ci   M10_dropcase_abgr   M11_null (the pre-fix
   file wholesale)    M12_announce_prefix_dropped (the old literal removed from the text: kills
   `ycrcb attrib' -- an L4-shaped mutant; recorded because the prefix is load-bearing for the witness)
   ACCEPT rows: live (RGBA), ci8 put, rgb put, abgr put; growth mutants M9/M10 (a case dropped) and
   M8 (everything declined).
```

## THE QUESTIONS — VERDICT LINE FIRST, then answer extensionally
Q1. THE PREMISE AUDIT, as questions. (a) Does `ok` really gate the ONLY store in the PUT branch, and does the
    GET branch remain byte-identical? (b) With `ok == 0`, is `buf` read anywhere before the function returns?
    (c) Is 1 << 12 free in `enum` (no other user of bit 12 of `d->warned` anywhere in `dev_sgi_re.c`)?
    (d) Does keeping the old literal as a prefix leave any OTHER probe or gate that keyed on the old
    per-pixel text now silent (grep `regress/` for `color mode =`)? (e) `ci8 put` wants 0xdd223344: is the
    ip32 word read big-endian so byte 0 is the top byte, and does an 8 bpp PUT store exactly one byte?

    YCRCB was refused for want of a packing spec -- is that still right?)

## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

Same family as `#504`, same function,\ndifferent arm -- *related by idiom, not by the one-cell-repair pattern.*\n\n**2. FULLY GUEST-REACHABLE, established by reading rather than assumed.**  `color_mode = mode &\nDE_MODE_TYPE_MASK`, and `mode` is the caller's `src_mode`/`dst_mode`, read straight out of `de_reg[]`\nfrom `CRIME_DE_MODE_SRC`/`CRIME_DE_MODE_DST` with **no filtering anywhere on the path**.  The four\nimplemented types are 0x00/0x10/0x20/0x30; `DE_MODE_TYPE_YCRCB` is **0xf0 and is documented in\n`crmfbreg.h`** but unimplemented, so a guest selecting a real, named mode reaches this arm.\n\n**3. A WITNESS IS ONE CONSTANT AWAY.**  `#505` built `regress/sgi_re_rgb_probe.py`,\na rung-3 probe that drives an XFER copy on an unmodified `-E sgi -e ip32` with both ladder controls and a\nsibling accept row, and whose `setup()` now takes **both** src and dst modes.  Pointing its `dst_mode` at\n`LIN_A | BUFDEPTH_32 | 0xf0` puts YCRCB into the PUT switch and reaches this arm.\n\n  *** THE ORACLE IS THE HARD PART AND IT NEEDS SAYING BEFORE THE ROUND STARTS: THE DEFECT'S OUTPUT IS\n  RANDOM, SO NO VALUE ROW CAN PIN IT. ***  `#504` hit exactly this and its answer is the precedent --\n  assert that the destination still holds its PRE-PAINT value, i.e. that the write was DECLINED, rather\n  than trying to assert anything about what was written.  A determinism oracle is refuted in advance by\n  this project's own record: two seats showed on `#504` that stack reuse can make a wrong value\n  deterministic, so *\"the same value twice\"* proves nothing.\n\n**FIX SHAPE: the option set has a member already chosen for its sibling.**  `#504` used DECLINE + LATCHED\nANNOUNCE with its own bit (`SGIRE_W_BADMODE = 1 << 10`), for the reason `#498` measured -- the four\nhandlers share one struct, so a shared latch is silenced by the other site's ordinary traffic.  **This arm\nneeds its OWN bit, `1 << 11`**, and `#504`'s comment already says the enum ends there on purpose so the\nnext maintainer finds it.  *That is a transferred in-tree warrant, not a \"cannot hurt\" addition.*\n\n*** PASS-1 AUDIT, 2026-09-07 (codex, `regress/artifacts/sgirandomcolour/codex_pass1_20260907.txt`).  TWO OF MY PREMISES WERE WRONG AND THE\nDISPOSITION SURVIVED. ***\n\n**VERDICT:** *\"DECLINE + LATCHED ANNOUNCE is justified, with an unconditional return from the PUT\ndefault.  But the brief misstates #504's oracle and the write's byte count; depth 8 also exposes\nuninitialized buffer bytes.\"*\n\n*** CORRECTION 1 -- THE ORACLE.  THIS IS THE ONE THAT WOULD HAVE COST A ROUND. ***  My brief said\n`#504`'s precedent asserts *the destination still holds its PRE-PAINT value*.  It does not.  The seat\nopened the probe: `lin_b is fg` expects **`FG_VAL = 0x77665544`**, not the pre-paint `0x11111111`,\nbecause a declined source GET **preserves the caller's foreground value**, which the following PUT\nthen writes.  In the seat's words, *\"the probe explicitly rejects preserving the destination as a\ndifferent disposition.\"*\n\n  **So `#504` warrants the latch-and-return IDIOM and does NOT supply the unchanged-destination\n  ORACLE I attributed to it.**  Those are different transfers and I ran them together.\n\n  **THE ORACLE THE SEAT PROPOSES INSTEAD, and it is stronger than either:** observe **ZERO\n  `MEM_WRITE` CALLS** from an unsupported PUT, *alongside* unchanged destination bytes -- because\n  that pair **distinguishes \"no write\" from \"same final value\"**, which neither assertion does\n  alone.  It also notes that if removing the RNG use is contractual, that must be observed\n  SEPARATELY.\n\n  *And a sentinel value will not rescue a value oracle here:* each assignment converts a SEPARATE\n  `random()` result to `uint8_t`, so the whole-word improbability argument `#504`'s old witness used\n  does not establish an impossible byte pattern.\n\n*** CORRECTION 2 -- THE BYTE COUNT, and it is the SAME WIDTH-AXIS LESSON `#505` PAID FOR HOURS\nEARLIER. ***  My brief said *four* bytes of `random()` are written.  The write is `bufdepth` bytes,\nnot four: at bufdepth 8 the arm fills `buf[0..3]` with RNG and ships **four further UNINITIALISED\nstack bytes** with them; at bufdepth 1 or 2 fewer than four RNG bytes land.  **\"Four\" is true only\nat bufdepth 4** -- the width every example happens to use.  *That is the exact shape of `#505`'s\nclause-(d) floor breach, arriving in the next round's brief the same day, written by the person who\nhad just repaired it.*  Overlaps [[sgirebufuninit]]; the round must say which defect owns which\nbytes.\n\n**OPTION SET: CONFIRMED COMPLETE, and the grep found three in-tree alternatives worth naming:**\n  * the helper's own GET default already provides a raw ABGR-shaped fallback (`dev_sgi_re.c:358`);\n  * **PCC2 has a real ACCEPT precedent** (`dev_pcc2.c:427`) -- a read served from backing storage\n    before the default diagnostic;\n  * **SGI's ROP diagnostics latch BY VALUE** (`dev_sgi_re.c:850`) rather than one bit per site.\n\nNone establishes correct YCRCB PUT semantics.  *\"Mirroring the GET fallback would repeat the\nunsupported-authority problem you warned about.  The YCRCB name establishes an encoding; it does not\nestablish packing, conversion, or depth semantics.\"*  IMPLEMENT is defensible in principle but no\npermitted source supplies an implementation or a demonstrated driver selection.  **No additional\nwarranted disposition outside the option set** -- which is the option-set rule returning a clean\nnegative for once, and that is worth recording as such.\n\n**THE LATCH BIT IS CONFIRMED AVAILABLE AND THE OBJECTION TO ONE BIT IS RECORDED:** occupied bits\nenumerate as 0..10, `warned` is `uint32_t`, so **`1 << 11` is free**.  The strongest objection is\ndiagnostic coverage -- *an earlier unnamed mode suppresses a later YCRCB announcement* -- and\nper-mode latching has in-tree precedent (the ROP site above).  One bit stays consistent with this\nhelper's existing policy, so it is a judgement the round must make explicitly rather than inherit.\n\n**FIX SHAPE (Q2), in the seat's words:** *a terminal default arm containing a conditional\nannouncement followed by an **unconditional function return***.  Note this is the SKETCH RULE's\nterritory -- the PUT branch's write sits after the switch and is shared by all five arms, so the\nreturn must be checked against the whole enclosing function before it is written.\n\n*** AND A WARNING THAT SAVES THE ROUND A FALSE START: `#504`'s PROBE CANNOT BE REUSED UNCHANGED. ***\n*\"#504's `lin_b is fg` remains green under edits confined to this colour default.  Both of that\nprobe's mode constants use RGBA, so it never selects the PUT colour default.  Its setup cannot be\ntransferred unchanged.\"*  `#505`'s probe is the better base -- its `setup()` already takes both src\nand dst modes -- but the same check applies to it and must be run, not assumed.\n\n**ENUMERATION CONFIRMED, with a qualification I did not have:** the twelve default selections are\neleven unnamed (`0x40`..`0xe0`) plus the named `0xf0` YCRCB, and they reach the arm *\"when the\npreceding coordinate/addressing/tile-validity checks pass\"*.\n\n*** THE ORACLE QUESTION, ANSWERED BEFORE THE ROUND STARTED -- AND THE NAIVE IMPLEMENTATION WOULD\nHAVE PRODUCED A FALSE GREEN.  codex, `regress/artifacts/sgirandomcolour/codex_oracle_20260907.txt`.\n***\n\n**VERDICT:** *\"Your distinction is conditionally sound.  A correctly scoped write-attempt observer\nworks for Round A; **the existing stepped probe supplies none.**  This fork's debugger watchpoints\noffer a qualified alternative.\"*\n\n**MY DISTINCTION SURVIVES, with a precision I had not stated.**  A count separates *no request* from\n*a request carrying the existing bytes* -- but *\"zero calls does not establish correct rejection\nunless the unsupported arm was ACTUALLY REACHED: an earlier return or an operation that never ran\nalso produces zero.\"*  That is the attribution problem, and it means the round owes a reach control\nbeside the count.\n\n*** THE MECHANISM EXISTS: debugger WRITE WATCHPOINTS, checked in the common memory path BEFORE\ndevice/cache/RAM dispatch, and neither PHYSICAL nor NO_EXCEPTIONS excludes this helper's write.  A\npty driver can observe them with NO instrumented build. ***  (`memory_rw.c:273`.)\n\n*** AND IT HAS TWO DECISIVE LIMITS, THE FIRST OF WHICH WOULD HAVE PRODUCED A FALSE GREEN: ***\n  1. **The check requires `!single_step`, and the existing probe's `run_ops()` uses `step`.**  So\n     *\"merely adding watchpoints and counting their messages to that execution scheme could report\n     ZERO DESPITE WRITES.\"*  The row would have reported \"no write happened\" while writes happened.\n     **A watchpoint oracle therefore requires the FREE-RUNNING probe shape, not the stepped one.**\n  2. It also requires `!about_to_enter_single_step` and **SETS that flag after its first report**, so\n     later pixel writes inside the same drawing command go unreported.  What Python would count is\n     *printed watchpoint reports*, not `MEM_WRITE` calls -- watchpoints carry no hit count.\n\n**SO THE ACHIEVABLE CLAIM IS BOUNDED, and the round must say so rather than overclaim:** the\nmechanism supports an **any-attempt-to-this-range** witness when observation is active throughout the\nrelevant execution.  It does NOT support *\"exactly N calls\"* or *\"no writes anywhere from this\nhelper\"*.  The conclusion must read **\"no observed write attempt to this range under these validated\nconditions\"** -- watching the destination cannot exclude a write to an UNINTENDED address, which is\nexactly the failure mode [[sgirebufuninit]] and the `#505` tiled row both hit in other forms.\n\n**THE SHIPPABLE DESIGN, in the seat's own construction:** verified arm reach and completed execution,\nan active observer that DOES detect a supported write to the same range (the liveness half), then no\nmatching write report for the rejected operation, with unchanged verified destination bytes as\ncorroboration and the announce count testing \"announce once\" separately.  **That is four rows, not\none**, and knowing it now is worth more than discovering it after the fix.\n\n*This is the carrier's `!single_step` family arriving through a NEW mechanism.  The carrier records\nthat a stepped probe cannot observe INTERRUPT DELIVERY (`cpu_dyntrans.c`'s\n\"do not cause interrupts while single-stepping\").  The WATCHPOINT case is the same guard on a\ndifferent feature and was not written down.*\n\n*** CODEX DESIGN PASS 2026-09-07 (`regress/artifacts/sgirandomcolour/codex_design_20260907.txt`).\nVERDICT: REVISE -- DECLINE + LATCHED ANNOUNCE **with a `return` before the PUT transfer**.  It\nrefuted TWO of the brief's premises, and one of them was the brief's central design claim. ***\n\n**S3 WAS WRONG, AND IT IS THE PREMISE THE WHOLE 'COPY THE SIBLING' READING RESTED ON.**  I wrote that\n`sgirandompixel` fixed *the GET side of this same helper*.  It did not: **it repaired the\nADDRESSING-MODE default at `:235`, which sits BEFORE GET and PUT diverge and returns for both\ndirections.  The COLOUR GET default at `:358` still performs a raw conversion.**  Two different\narms -- so the sibling is a precedent for the DISPOSITION and not for the site, and its comment\ncannot simply be copied.\n\n**S5 WAS WRONG, AND THIS ONE UNBLOCKS THE ROUND.**  I claimed the decline's 'what does `buf` hold'\nquestion was the SAME question as [[sgirebufuninit]]'s, making that row a prerequisite.  codex:\n*\"A declined PUT need not leave anything defined in `buf`, provided nothing consumes it.  The helper\nitself owns the memory write.  Its callers receive no buffer to transfer afterward.\"*\n\n    remove the fill, fall through   -> transfers indeterminate stack bytes   REJECT\n    zero the span, fall through     -> overwrites destination with zero      needs a warrant\n    RETURN from the PUT default     -> preserves destination bytes           PREFERRED\n\n> **So the two rows are INDEPENDENT and there is no patch ordering.**  This round decides whether an\n> unsupported PUT writes anything at all; `sgirebufuninit` decides how paths that DO transfer avoid\n> consuming uninitialised bytes.  *Initialising the buffer would neither remove the four `random()`\n> calls nor justify writing zeros for an unsupported format.*\n\n**S1 needs a width qualification:** four bytes are ASSIGNED randomly, but the transfer length is 1, 2,\n4 or 8.  A narrow transfer exposes only the prefix; an eight-byte transfer additionally consumes four\nuninitialised bytes.\n\n**S2's inference was a CORRELATE and the real population is twelve, not one.**  `grep -c YCRCB` = 0\nproves only that one NAMED constant is absent.  Enumerating `mode & 0xf0`: sixteen values, **four\nexplicit PUT cases (CI/RGB/RGBA/ABGR) and TWELVE reaching `default:`** -- eleven unnamed plus YCRCB.\nThe DE register handler stores the mode without filtering the type nibble, and both line and rectangle\ncallers pass it through unchanged.  *The named-constant search was the same error that cost the\n`asicbadaddr` census a 4x undercount.*\n\n**FAULT IS AVAILABLE HERE and my brief implied otherwise** -- `memory_rw.c:603` delivers MIPS\nexceptions.  codex rejects it anyway on better grounds than availability: the helper returns `void`,\nits transfers request `NO_EXCEPTIONS`, and the outer device handler returns success, so FAULT would\nneed an error-propagation design and a warrant for the guest-visible exception.\n\n**THE LATCH MUST BE AN INDEPENDENT BIT, NOT `SGIRE_W_BADMODE`** -- sharing it would let an ADDRESSING\nfailure suppress the COLOUR diagnostic.  That is `#490`'s measured latch-collision class, predicted\nhere before the code exists.\n\n**Q4, the predicted escape:** condition the new `return` on `bufdepth == 4` -- the canonical clause-(d)\nshape, conditioning the repair on a field the transfer consumes.  **codex expects `rgb tiled64` to stay\ngreen under it**, because that row varies the GET SOURCE's addressing and width while keeping a linear\nfour-byte RGBA destination: *\"its name does not establish PUT coverage.\"*  The proposed closure is the\ndestination axes together -- twelve unsupported types x four widths x four address modes = **192\ncombinations** -- with whole-span checks including eight-byte spans.  *Source-based prediction; no\nmutant built.*\n\n**Q3, the witness:** source inspection supports a rung-3 cold-debugger probe on stock IP32 and codex\nfound no machine-description blocker.  But **the sibling probe's green rows are NOT a witness for this\nrow**: its setup fixes the DESTINATION to RGBA, so varying its source-mode argument never exercises the\ncolour PUT default.  Record **'source-reachable; rung-3 attempt pending'** -- codex ran nothing, as\ninstructed, so the attempted-probe prerequisite is NOT yet discharged.\n\n*** A CONSEQUENCE OF THE DECLINE DESIGN, found by the codex seat on the SIBLING brief\n(`sgimtedepth4dead`) and cross-filed here because it is this row's design that it bears on. ***\n\n*\"A helper return before accessing the buffer leaves the caller's colour unchanged.  MTE still\nperforms the dependent PUT, and its `bg` persists across iterations -- so a declined GET can reuse an\nearlier source value.\"*\n\n**That is distinct from buffer initialisation and it bites the agreed repair.**  The design settled\nfor this row is DECLINE + LATCHED ANNOUNCE with a `return` before the transfer -- and on the MTE\npath the caller's `bg` is a loop-scope variable, so a declined pixel does not leave the destination\nuntouched: **it writes the PREVIOUS pixel's colour.**  A decline that silently smears the last good\nvalue across a run of unsupported pixels is a different wrong answer from `random()`, and arguably a\nmore plausible-looking one.\n\n*The round owes an answer: does the decline return before the PUT as well, and if so what does the\ndestination hold?  This was not visible from this row's own brief, which framed the question around\n`buf` rather than around the caller's colour variable.*\n\n*** DESIGN PASS 2, 2026-09-07: I CONFLATED A DECLINED GET WITH A DECLINED PUT.  PASS 1's DESIGN\nSTANDS UNCHANGED. ***\n\nThe PUT-default `return` exits **before** `memory_rw(... MEM_WRITE ...)`, so whatever the caller's\n`color` holds, that declined PUT does not transfer it.  Leaving the destination's existing bytes\nintact is the honest disposition for an unsupported write.  **The `bg` observation is about the\ndependent-PUT problem, which is a different row.**\n\n**AND MY S4 DID NOT ESTABLISH REACHABILITY:** MTE builds modes from addressing and depth bits only,\nleaving the colour type at CI, and its one colour override sits behind the unreachable `depth == 4`.\n**Neither construction reaches this PUT colour default**, so the MTE `bg` behaviour does not bear on\nthis row at all.\n\n*Two further qualifications on my wording: MTE reloads `bg` from `CRIME_MTE_BG` on **each transfer\ninvocation**, so a declined GET retains the latest value from THAT invocation or the register's\ninitial value -- never a previous command's local `bg`.  And \"the whole rectangle deterministically\nreceives the last good colour\" is too strong independently, because CI's accepted PUT initialises\nonly `buf[0]` and wider depths consume uninitialised bytes.*\n\n**THE LATCH GRANULARITY IS CONFIRMED AS APPROPRIATE AND ITS LIMIT STATED:** one message per device\ninstance for the latch's lifetime -- so a thousand declined pixels produce one line, **and none if an\nearlier command already set the bit** (the struct is zeroed at init; there is no per-command reset).\nIt deliberately cannot distinguish one declined pixel from a rectangle; reporting extent would need\ncounting or a command summary.\n\n**RESIDUAL FOUND: bounding the PUT warning does not bound every warning from the operation.**  The\n**GET colour default still logs without a latch**, and DE can perform that GET for a\ndestination-dependent raster operation before ever reaching the declined PUT.  So a round that\nlatches only the PUT arm leaves a guest-drivable unlatched diagnostic in the same code path.\n\n**THE CLAUSE-(d) MUTANT:** change the new `return;` to `break;`, keeping the latch and the\nannouncement.  Execution resumes at the memory write and consumes the uninitialised buffer -- **it\nbreaks the no-write contract without restoring `random()`**.  `rgb samemode` and `rgb tiled64` both\nexpected to stay green (supported RGB/RGBA, never reaching the default).  *The coverage gap is wider\nthan MTE: the scoped scripts never select an unsupported destination colour type at all, so their\ngreen results cannot establish this decline.*\n\n*** THE WITNESS PROBLEM IS CHARACTERISED, AND IT IS HARDER THAN THE ROW ASSUMED.  From the codex\nORACLE answer (`regress/artifacts/sgirandomcolour/codex_oracle_20260907.txt`), read in full\n2026-09-07 -- most of this had been sitting unread. ***\n\n**TWO PREMISE CORRECTIONS FIRST, and the second changes the FIX, not just the witness:**\n\n1. *\"The buffer lacks a declaration initializer; it is not NEVER INITIALIZED.  PUT arms assign\n   prefixes, and GET fills the requested transfer length.\"*  The row's shorthand was sloppy.\n2. *** \"THE MISSING `break` IS NOT WHAT PERMITS THE WRITE.  The write is AFTER the switch, so adding\n   `break` would still reach it.  **The proposed return matters.**\" ***  A `break`-shaped repair\n   would look right and do nothing -- exactly the sketch-versus-enclosing-control-flow rule this\n   project minted after four readers proposed a `return` into a function with a common tail.\n\n*** THE OBSERVER EXISTS, AND IT IS DISQUALIFIED BY THE PROBE'S OWN EXECUTION MODE. ***\n\nThe fork has debugger WRITE-WATCHPOINTS, and the common memory path checks them **before**\ndevice/cache/RAM dispatch -- *\"neither `PHYSICAL` nor `NO_EXCEPTIONS` excludes this helper's\nwrite\"* (`memory_rw.c:273`).  A matching attempt prints `WATCHPOINT: write`, which a pty driver can\ncount with no instrumented build.  **But two conditions kill it as written:**\n\n    memory_rw.c:273   the check requires  !single_step\n                      -- and sgi_re_randompixel_probe.py's run_ops() uses `step'.  Adding\n                      watchpoints to that scheme \"could report ZERO DESPITE WRITES.\"\n\n    same check        also requires  !about_to_enter_single_step, and SETS that flag after its\n                      first report -- so subsequent pixel writes inside the SAME drawing command\n                      go unreported.\n\n> **This is the carrier's single-stepping rule arriving on a new mechanism.**  It already records\n> that a stepped probe cannot observe interrupt DELIVERY (`cpu_dyntrans.c`: *\"Do not cause\n> interrupts while single-stepping\"*).  **Watchpoints are a second capability gated the same way**,\n> and the same discriminator applies: ask what the observation depends on before designing around\n> it.\n\n**WHAT THE MECHANISM CAN AND CANNOT SUPPORT, in codex's words:** it supports an\n**any-attempt-to-this-range** witness *\"when observation is active throughout the relevant\nexecution\"* -- i.e. FREE-RUNNING, not stepped -- and even then only the first report survives the\nflag.  It *\"cannot automatically support 'exactly N calls', or 'no writes anywhere from this\nhelper'.  A literal, attributed call count needs a suitable host-side observer or `harness:`\nwitness.\"*\n\n*** AND Q3 DEMOLISHES THE CHEAP ORACLE: \"unchanged destination bytes prove only unchanged OBSERVED\nCONTENTS.\" ***  Equality at the observation points is also consistent with a same-value write, a\nwrite followed by restoration, a write to somewhere else, no drawing operation at all, or an earlier\nhelper return.  **Two of those are live here rather than theoretical**: the helper returns on an\ninvalid tile pointer (`dev_sgi_re.c:274`), and *\"the memory path can discard an out-of-RAM write\nwhile returning success; corresponding reads can return zeros\"* (`memory_rw.c:662`).\n\n> **So a destination-equality row needs a control establishing that the pre-paint REALLY EXISTS and\n> that a SUPPORTED operation through the same path actually lands** -- otherwise \"unchanged\" is\n> indistinguishable from \"nothing ever happened\", which is the vacuity shape in its purest form.\n\n**THE TWO VIABLE PATHS, neither cheap:**\n* **(a) a FREE-RUNNING probe with a write-watchpoint** -- any-attempt semantics, first report only,\n  and it must abandon the stepped `run_ops()` idiom the sibling probes use;\n* **(b) a `harness:` witness with a host-side observer** -- an attributed call count, which the\n  ladder admits only when the round's subject IS the apparatus, so it would need care not to become\n  the rung-1 shortcut the ladder exists to refuse.\n\n**STATUS: this row is NOT held for fable5 and is the only HIGH row that is not** -- but its blocker\nis now a characterised capability gap rather than an open question, and neither path is a session's\nwork.  *Recorded in full because most of this analysis had been sitting unread in the artifact since\nit was produced, which is this session's most-repeated failure.*\n\n*** DESIGN PASS 1, 2026-09-08.  FIVE SEATS, UNANIMOUS ON BOTH QUESTIONS -- and the strongest answer\nREFUSED TO CERTIFY ITSELF, which is the part worth keeping. ***\n\n**THE DISPOSITION: member 3, DECLINE + LATCHED ANNOUNCE, implemented as candidate (b) -- skip the\nPUT's `memory_rw` for a declined mode.  Never an early `return`.**\n\n*** grok's REFRAMING IS BETTER THAN MY OPTION LIST AND IT DISSOLVES THE QUESTION THE ROW WAS FILED\nON. ***  The row asked *\"what should a declined BUFFER FILL leave behind?\"*, treating `buf` as the\nanalogue of `#504`'s `*color`.  It is not:\n\n> *\"Destination of a PUT is guest memory at `tileptr + ofs`, not `buf`.  `#504` declined the\n> helper's OUTPUT.  The PUT output is the store.  Skipping the store leaves whatever the guest\n> already had -- deterministic, no invented colour, no host bytes.  `buf` can stay uninitialised\n> because it is not sent.\"*\n\nSo the fix is not a fill policy at all.  **The question \"what should the buffer contain\" was the\nwrong question**, and my option (a) (zero-fill) was demoted to a fallback for exactly one case:\nif some tail consumes `buf` or assumes the store happened.\n\n**AND IT CORRECTED MY (a) IN PASSING:** zero-filling only `buf[0..3]` to mirror the `random()` shape\nis NOT (a) -- `bufdepth` can be 8, so that form *\"is this arm already instantiating\n`sgirebufuninit`\"*.  If (a) is ever taken it must define the whole payload.\n\n*** IT DECLINED TO CERTIFY (b), NAMING EXACTLY THE GAP: *** *\"That tail is not in the packet.  I\nwill not guess `x++`, a dirty bit, a return value, or a use of `buf`.  I cannot certify (b) against\n'nine lines down.'\"*  That is this project's own sketch rule, applied by a seat to its own\nrecommendation, unprompted beyond the caution in the brief.\n\n## *** BOTH OF ITS UNCHECKABLE PREMISES ARE NOW SETTLED BY ME, BY OPENING THE FILE ***\n\n**1. THERE IS NO COMMON TAIL.**  `horrible_getputpixel()` is `void`, and after the PUT store the\n`else` runs the GET path and the function ENDS -- `}` closing the else, `}` closing the function,\nnothing between them.  No dirty bit, no counter, no return value, no later use of `buf`.\n**So (b) is safe**, and the `put_ok` flag form grok gave is belt-and-braces rather than necessary --\nwhich is still the right way to write it, because it cannot become the early-`return` trap under a\nlater edit.\n\n**2. `fatal()` DOES NOT EXIT.**  `src/core/debugmsg.c`: `va_start; va_debug; va_end;` and return --\nno `exit()`, no early-out.  grok flagged that if `fatal()` aborted, *\"the random fill is dead and\nthe guest-visible row collapses.\"*  It does not abort, so **the random bytes really do reach\n`memory_rw(..., MEM_WRITE)` and the defect stands.**\n\n*That premise should have been IN the packet.  The carrier records `fatal()`'s non-exit as a\nstanding fact and I did not inline it -- the settled-premises rule exists precisely so a packet-fed\nseat need not park on something already known.*\n\n## ROUND SCOPE: TWO ROUNDS, 5 of 5, each with its own reason\n\nNot one round with `sgirebufuninit`, despite the two sitting in the same `switch`.  **minimax gave\nthe sharpest form:** *\"a regression in the CI/depth-8 path and a regression in the YCRCB path\nproduce the same test failure\"* -- i.e. one oracle cannot tell them apart, which is exactly the\nround-scope rule's test rather than a preference.  grok: *\"They sit in the same `switch`.  That is\nnot the test.\"*  **And it named the trap explicitly: zero-initialising `buf` at its declaration\nwould silently convert this round's (b) into (a) and smuggle `sgirebufuninit`'s fix in with it.**\n\n*This was asked as an open question arguing both ways, so the unanimity is worth something -- unlike\nunanimity on a brief-supplied FACT, which this project has measured to be worth nothing.*\n\n## RESIDUALS RAISED, NOT YET DISPOSED\n\n* grok: the arm *\"is already confused -- it announces unimplemented (`TODO` + `fatal`) and then\n  IMPLEMENTS with noise.  The announce is the right instinct (unlatched, per-pixel, therefore wrong\n  shape).  The fill is the defect.\"*  So the existing `fatal()` becomes the latched announce; it is\n  not added, it is fixed.\n* **12 of 16 mode values reach `default:` and only `0xf0` is documented.**  grok accepted the\n  arithmetic (`16 - 4`, needing no header) but declined the reachability claim as *\"claims about\n  other code, not in the packet\"* -- correctly.  **A round taking this owes the reachability\n  check**, and `IMPLEMENT` (member 7) for YCRCB is a separate design: *\"no YCbCr packing,\n  subsample, or byte order in this packet; 11 of the 12 values are not even named.\"*\n* Q4/Q5 (detector and witness) are in the seat answers and NOT yet consolidated here -- that is an\n  UNREAD-REVIEW debt on this row until it is.\n\n*** THE Q4/Q5 UNREAD-REVIEW DEBT IS DISCHARGED, AND IT WAS WORTH PAYING: THE PANEL'S OWN MAJORITY\nDETECTOR WOULD HAVE SHIPPED AN ESCAPABLE ROW. ***\n\nI recorded the detector and witness answers as unread.  Reading all five:\n\n## THE DETECTOR -- convergent, and it is a DESTINATION CANARY, not \"looks non-random\"\n\nPre-fill the target bytes with a distinctive non-zero canary, execute ONE PUT with a declined mode,\nread back, and **pass iff the canary is bit-identical** -- i.e. the oracle is *the store did not\nhappen*, not *the value is not random*.  A liveness control in the SAME script repeats with a\ndefined mode (RGBA or CI) and the canary MUST change; without it a dead PUT path greens the round.\n\n*** THE HOLE, and three of the five seats were in it: DO NOT PIN THE TEST TO `0xf0` ALONE. ***\ndeepseek and kimi both proposed YCRCB `0xf0` as *the* test mode, kimi arguing it is the value a real\nguest would plausibly select.  grok:\n\n> *\"A mutant `case 0xf0: put_ok = 0; break;` with `default:` still random would escape.  Use at\n> least one UNDOCUMENTED value (`0x40` is the first hole) as the primary; `0xf0` can be a second\n> vector, not the only one.\"*\n\n**That is a real escape against the majority design.**  The documented mode is the most tempting\nchoice and the weakest one, because a mutant can special-case exactly it.\n\n**FOUR MORE STRENGTHENINGS, one per seat, none of them duplicated:**\n* **grok** -- vary `bufdepth` over at least {1, 4, 8}, so a mutant that skips the store only when\n  `bufdepth == 4` dies.\n* **minimax** -- make the oracle **CONJUNCTIVE**: the latch transitioned 0 -> 1 *during that pixel*\n  (record the PRE-state) AND `memory_rw` was not called for it.  Either conjunct alone is escapable,\n  and recording the pre-state defeats a mutant that pre-sets the latch at init.\n* **kimi** -- assert the announce count is **exactly 1**, which is what actually pins the latch.\n* **glm** -- assert the ADJACENT bytes are unchanged too, since the write is only `bufdepth` bytes\n  at `ofs`; and run twice asserting identical transcripts.\n\n**AND grok'S HONEST LIMIT ON ITS OWN ORACLE:** keeping `buf[i] = random()` while setting\n`put_ok = 0` leaves the canary intact, so a destination-only oracle cannot see the leftover\n`random()` calls. That asymmetry is itself worth carrying into the design: the two default\narms of one function already disagree about what an unhandled colour type means.\n\nNote this is a DIFFERENT defect from [[sgirebufuninit]] at the same lines: that one is about bytes NOBODY\nassigns; this is about bytes assigned from `random()'. This row is the\n**colour-mode** default of a different `switch` in the same function:\n\n    default:buf[0] = random();  buf[1] = random();\n            buf[2] = random();  buf[3] = random();\n            // TODO.\n            fatal(\"[ put: color mode = 0x%x ]\\n\", color_mode);\n    }\n    cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, tileptr + ofs, buf, bufdepth, MEM_WRITE, NO_EXCEPTIONS|PHYSICAL);\n\n**There is no `break` and no decline: control falls straight into the write**, so four bytes of host\n`random()` land in guest memory at a guest-chosen tile offset. A determinism oracle is refuted in advance by\n  this project's own record: two seats showed on `#504` that stack reuse can make a wrong value\n  deterministic, so *\"the same value twice\"* proves nothing.\n\n**FIX SHAPE: the option set has a member already chosen for its sibling.**  `#504` used DECLINE + LATCHED\nANNOUNCE with its own bit (`SGIRE_W_BADMODE = 1 << 10`), for the reason `#498` measured -- the four\nhandlers share one struct, so a shared latch is silenced by the other site's ordinary traffic.  **This arm\nneeds its OWN bit, `1 << 11`**, and `#504`'s comment already says the enum ends there on purpose so the\nnext maintainer finds it.  *That is a transferred in-tree warrant, not a \"cannot hurt\" addition.*\n\n*** PASS-1 AUDIT, 2026-09-07 (codex, `regress/artifacts/sgirandomcolour/codex_pass1_20260907.txt`).  TWO OF MY PREMISES WERE WRONG AND THE\nDISPOSITION SURVIVED. ***\n\n**VERDICT:** *\"DECLINE + LATCHED ANNOUNCE is justified, with an unconditional return from the PUT\ndefault. THIS IS THE ONE THAT WOULD HAVE COST A ROUND. ***  My brief said\n`#504`'s precedent asserts *the destination still holds its PRE-PAINT value*.  It does not.  The seat\nopened the probe: `lin_b is fg` expects **`FG_VAL = 0x77665544`**, not the pre-paint `0x11111111`,\nbecause a declined source GET **preserves the caller's foreground value**, which the following PUT\nthen writes.  In the seat's words, *\"the probe explicitly rejects preserving the destination as a\ndifferent disposition.\"*\n\n  **So `#504` warrants the latch-and-return IDIOM and does NOT supply the unchanged-destination\n  ORACLE I attributed to it.**  Those are different transfers and I ran them together.\n\n  **THE ORACLE THE SEAT PROPOSES INSTEAD, and it is stronger than either:** observe **ZERO\n  `MEM_WRITE` CALLS** from an unsupported PUT, *alongside* unchanged destination bytes -- because\n  that pair **distinguishes \"no write\" from \"same final value\"**, which neither assertion does\n  alone.  It also notes that if removing the RNG use is contractual, that must be observed\n  SEPARATELY.\n\n  *And a sentinel value will not rescue a value oracle here:* each assignment converts a SEPARATE\n  `random()` result to `uint8_t`, so the whole-word improbability argument `#504`'s old witness used\n  does not establish an impossible byte pattern.\n\n*** CORRECTION 2 -- THE BYTE COUNT, and it is the SAME WIDTH-AXIS LESSON `#505` PAID FOR HOURS\nEARLIER. ***  My brief said *four* bytes of `random()` are written.  The write is `bufdepth` bytes,\nnot four: at bufdepth 8 the arm fills `buf[0..3]` with RNG and ships **four further UNINITIALISED\nstack bytes** with them; at bufdepth 1 or 2 fewer than four RNG bytes land.  **\"Four\" is true only\nat bufdepth 4** -- the width every example happens to use.  *That is the exact shape of `#505`'s\nclause-(d) floor breach, arriving in the next round's brief the same day, written by the person who\nhad just repaired it.*  Overlaps [[sgirebufuninit]]; the round must say which defect owns which\nbytes.\n\n**OPTION SET: CONFIRMED COMPLETE, and the grep found three in-tree alternatives worth naming:**\n  * the helper's own GET default already provides a raw ABGR-shaped fallback (`dev_sgi_re.c:358`);\n  * **PCC2 has a real ACCEPT precedent** (`dev_pcc2.c:427`) -- a read served from backing storage\n    before the default diagnostic;\n  * **SGI's ROP diagnostics latch BY VALUE** (`dev_sgi_re.c:850`) rather than one bit per site.\n\nNone establishes correct YCRCB PUT semantics.  *\"Mirroring the GET fallback would repeat the\nunsupported-authority problem you warned about.  The YCRCB name establishes an encoding; it does not\nestablish packing, conversion, or depth semantics.\"*  IMPLEMENT is defensible in principle but no\npermitted source supplies an implementation or a demonstrated driver selection.  **No additional\nwarranted disposition outside the option set** -- which is the option-set rule returning a clean\nnegative for once, and that is worth recording as such.\n\n**THE LATCH BIT IS CONFIRMED AVAILABLE AND THE OBJECTION TO ONE BIT IS RECORDED:** occupied bits\nenumerate as 0..10, `warned` is `uint32_t`, so **`1 << 11` is free**.  The strongest objection is\ndiagnostic coverage -- *an earlier unnamed mode suppresses a later YCRCB announcement* -- and\nper-mode latching has in-tree precedent (the ROP site above).  One bit stays consistent with this\nhelper's existing policy, so it is a judgement the round must make explicitly rather than inherit.\n\n**FIX SHAPE (Q2), in the seat's words:** *a terminal default arm containing a conditional\nannouncement followed by an **unconditional function return***.  Note this is the SKETCH RULE's\nterritory -- the PUT branch's write sits after the switch and is shared by all five arms, so the\nreturn must be checked against the whole enclosing function before it is written.\n\n*** AND A WARNING THAT SAVES THE ROUND A FALSE START: `#504`'s PROBE CANNOT BE REUSED UNCHANGED. ***\n*\"#504's `lin_b is fg` remains green under edits confined to this colour default. Its setup cannot be\ntransferred unchanged.\"*  `#505`'s probe is the better base -- its `setup()` already takes both src\nand dst modes -- but the same check applies to it and must be run, not assumed.\n\n**ENUMERATION CONFIRMED, with a qualification I did not have:** the twelve default selections are\neleven unnamed (`0x40`..`0xe0`) plus the named `0xf0` YCRCB, and they reach the arm *\"when the\npreceding coordinate/addressing/tile-validity checks pass\"*.\n\n*** THE ORACLE QUESTION, ANSWERED BEFORE THE ROUND STARTED -- AND THE NAIVE IMPLEMENTATION WOULD\nHAVE PRODUCED A FALSE GREEN.  codex, `regress/artifacts/sgirandomcolour/codex_oracle_20260907.txt`.\n***\n\n**VERDICT:** *\"Your distinction is conditionally sound.  A correctly scoped write-attempt observer\nworks for Round A; **the existing stepped probe supplies none.**  This fork's debugger watchpoints\noffer a qualified alternative.\"*\n\n**MY DISTINCTION SURVIVES, with a precision I had not stated.**  A count separates *no request* from\n*a request carrying the existing bytes* -- but *\"zero calls does not establish correct rejection\nunless the unsupported arm was ACTUALLY REACHED: an earlier return or an operation that never ran\nalso produces zero.\"*  That is the attribution problem, and it means the round owes a reach control\nbeside the count.\n\n*** THE MECHANISM EXISTS: debugger WRITE WATCHPOINTS, checked in the common memory path BEFORE\ndevice/cache/RAM dispatch, and neither PHYSICAL nor NO_EXCEPTIONS excludes this helper's write.  A\npty driver can observe them with NO instrumented build. ***  (`memory_rw.c:273`.)\n\n*** AND IT HAS TWO DECISIVE LIMITS, THE FIRST OF WHICH WOULD HAVE PRODUCED A FALSE GREEN: ***\n  1. **The check requires `!single_step`, and the existing probe's `run_ops()` uses `step`.**  So\n     *\"merely adding watchpoints and counting their messages to that execution scheme could report\n     ZERO DESPITE WRITES.\"*  The row would have reported \"no write happened\" while writes happened.\n     **A watchpoint oracle therefore requires the FREE-RUNNING probe shape, not the stepped one.**\n  2. It also requires `!about_to_enter_single_step` and **SETS that flag after its first report**, so\n     later pixel writes inside the same drawing command go unreported.  What Python would count is\n     *printed watchpoint reports*, not `MEM_WRITE` calls -- watchpoints carry no hit count.\n\n**SO THE ACHIEVABLE CLAIM IS BOUNDED, and the round must say so rather than overclaim:** the\nmechanism supports an **any-attempt-to-this-range** witness when observation is active throughout the\nrelevant execution.  It does NOT support *\"exactly N calls\"* or *\"no writes anywhere from this\nhelper\"*.  The conclusion must read **\"no observed write attempt to this range under these validated\nconditions\"** -- watching the destination cannot exclude a write to an UNINTENDED address, which is\nexactly the failure mode [[sgirebufuninit]] and the `#505` tiled row both hit in other forms.\n\n**THE SHIPPABLE DESIGN, in the seat's own construction:** verified arm reach and completed execution,\nan active observer that DOES detect a supported write to the same range (the liveness half), then no\nmatching write report for the rejected operation, with unchanged verified destination bytes as\ncorroboration and the announce count testing \"announce once\" separately.  **That is four rows, not\none**, and knowing it now is worth more than discovering it after the fix.\n\n*This is the carrier's `!single_step` family arriving through a NEW mechanism.  The carrier records\nthat a stepped probe cannot observe INTERRUPT DELIVERY (`cpu_dyntrans.c`'s\n\"do not cause interrupts while single-stepping\").  The WATCHPOINT case is the same guard on a\ndifferent feature and was not written down.*\n\n*** CODEX DESIGN PASS 2026-09-07 (`regress/artifacts/sgirandomcolour/codex_design_20260907.txt`).\nVERDICT: REVISE -- DECLINE + LATCHED ANNOUNCE **with a `return` before the PUT transfer**.  It\nrefuted TWO of the brief's premises, and one of them was the brief's central design claim. ***\n\n**S3 WAS WRONG, AND IT IS THE PREMISE THE WHOLE 'COPY THE SIBLING' READING RESTED ON.**  I wrote that\n`sgirandompixel` fixed *the GET side of this same helper*.  It did not: **it repaired the\nADDRESSING-MODE default at `:235`, which sits BEFORE GET and PUT diverge and returns for both\ndirections.  The COLOUR GET default at `:358` still performs a raw conversion.**  Two different\narms -- so the sibling is a precedent for the DISPOSITION and not for the site, and its comment\ncannot simply be copied.\n\n**S5 WAS WRONG, AND THIS ONE UNBLOCKS THE ROUND.**  I claimed the decline's 'what does `buf` hold'\nquestion was the SAME question as [[sgirebufuninit]]'s, making that row a prerequisite.  codex:\n*\"A declined PUT need not leave anything defined in `buf`, provided nothing consumes it.  The helper\nitself owns the memory write.  Its callers receive no buffer to transfer afterward.\"*\n\n    remove the fill, fall through   -> transfers indeterminate stack bytes   REJECT\n    zero the span, fall through     -> overwrites destination with zero      needs a warrant\n    RETURN from the PUT default     -> preserves destination bytes           PREFERRED\n\n> **So the two rows are INDEPENDENT and there is no patch ordering.**  This round decides whether an\n> unsupported PUT writes anything at all; `sgirebufuninit` decides how paths that DO transfer avoid\n> consuming uninitialised bytes.  *Initialising the buffer would neither remove the four `random()`\n> calls nor justify writing zeros for an unsupported format.*\n\n**S1 needs a width qualification:** four bytes are ASSIGNED randomly, but the transfer length is 1, 2,\n4 or 8.  A narrow transfer exposes only the prefix; an eight-byte transfer additionally consumes four\nuninitialised bytes.\n\n**S2's inference was a CORRELATE and the real population is twelve, not one.**  `grep -c YCRCB` = 0\nproves only that one NAMED constant is absent.  Enumerating `mode & 0xf0`: sixteen values, **four\nexplicit PUT cases (CI/RGB/RGBA/ABGR) and TWELVE reaching `default:`** -- eleven unnamed plus YCRCB.\nThe DE register handler stores the mode without filtering the type nibble, and both line and rectangle\ncallers pass it through unchanged.  *The named-constant search was the same error that cost the\n`asicbadaddr` census a 4x undercount.*\n\n**FAULT IS AVAILABLE HERE and my brief implied otherwise** -- `memory_rw.c:603` delivers MIPS\nexceptions.  codex rejects it anyway on better grounds than availability: the helper returns `void`,\nits transfers request `NO_EXCEPTIONS`, and the outer device handler returns success, so FAULT would\nneed an error-propagation design and a warrant for the guest-visible exception.\n\n**THE LATCH MUST BE AN INDEPENDENT BIT, NOT `SGIRE_W_BADMODE`** -- sharing it would let an ADDRESSING\nfailure suppress the COLOUR diagnostic.  That is `#490`'s measured latch-collision class, predicted\nhere before the code exists.\n\n**Q4, the predicted escape:** condition the new `return` on `bufdepth == 4` -- the canonical clause-(d)\nshape, conditioning the repair on a field the transfer consumes.  **codex expects `rgb tiled64` to stay\ngreen under it**, because that row varies the GET SOURCE's addressing and width while keeping a linear\nfour-byte RGBA destination: *\"its name does not establish PUT coverage.\"*  The proposed closure is the\ndestination axes together -- twelve unsupported types x four widths x four address modes = **192\ncombinations** -- with whole-span checks including eight-byte spans.  *Source-based prediction; no\nmutant built.*\n\n**Q3, the witness:** source inspection supports a rung-3 cold-debugger probe on stock IP32 and codex\nfound no machine-description blocker.  But **the sibling probe's green rows are NOT a witness for this\nrow**: its setup fixes the DESTINATION to RGBA, so varying its source-mode argument never exercises the\ncolour PUT default.  Record **'source-reachable; rung-3 attempt pending'** -- codex ran nothing, as\ninstructed, so the attempted-probe prerequisite is NOT yet discharged.\n\n*** A CONSEQUENCE OF THE DECLINE DESIGN, found by the codex seat on the SIBLING brief\n(`sgimtedepth4dead`) and cross-filed here because it is this row's design that it bears on. ***\n\n*\"A helper return before accessing the buffer leaves the caller's colour unchanged.  MTE still\nperforms the dependent PUT, and its `bg` persists across iterations -- so a declined GET can reuse an\nearlier source value.\"*\n\n**That is distinct from buffer initialisation and it bites the agreed repair.**  The design settled\nfor this row is DECLINE + LATCHED ANNOUNCE with a `return` before the transfer -- and on the MTE\npath the caller's `bg` is a loop-scope variable, so a declined pixel does not leave the destination\nuntouched: **it writes the PREVIOUS pixel's colour.**  A decline that silently smears the last good\nvalue across a run of unsupported pixels is a different wrong answer from `random()`, and arguably a\nmore plausible-looking one.\n\n*The round owes an answer: does the decline return before the PUT as well, and if so what does the\ndestination hold?  This was not visible from this row's own brief, which framed the question around\n`buf` rather than around the caller's colour variable.*\n\n*** DESIGN PASS 2, 2026-09-07: I CONFLATED A DECLINED GET WITH A DECLINED PUT.  PASS 1's DESIGN\nSTANDS UNCHANGED. ***\n\nThe PUT-default `return` exits **before** `memory_rw(... MEM_WRITE ...)`, so whatever the caller's\n`color` holds, that declined PUT does not transfer it.  Leaving the destination's existing bytes\nintact is the honest disposition for an unsupported write.  **The `bg` observation is about the\ndependent-PUT problem, which is a different row.**\n\n**AND MY S4 DID NOT ESTABLISH REACHABILITY:** MTE builds modes from addressing and depth bits only,\nleaving the colour type at CI, and its one colour override sits behind the unreachable `depth == 4`.\n**Neither construction reaches this PUT colour default**, so the MTE `bg` behaviour does not bear on\nthis row at all.\n\n*Two further qualifications on my wording: MTE reloads `bg` from `CRIME_MTE_BG` on **each transfer\ninvocation**, so a declined GET retains the latest value from THAT invocation or the register's\ninitial value -- never a previous command's local `bg`.  And \"the whole rectangle deterministically\nreceives the last good colour\" is too strong independently, because CI's accepted PUT initialises\nonly `buf[0]` and wider depths consume uninitialised bytes.*\n\n**THE LATCH GRANULARITY IS CONFIRMED AS APPROPRIATE AND ITS LIMIT STATED:** one message per device\ninstance for the latch's lifetime -- so a thousand declined pixels produce one line, **and none if an\nearlier command already set the bit** (the struct is zeroed at init; there is no per-command reset).\nIt deliberately cannot distinguish one declined pixel from a rectangle; reporting extent would need\ncounting or a command summary.\n\n**RESIDUAL FOUND: bounding the PUT warning does not bound every warning from the operation.**  The\n**GET colour default still logs without a latch**, and DE can perform that GET for a\ndestination-dependent raster operation before ever reaching the declined PUT.  So a round that\nlatches only the PUT arm leaves a guest-drivable unlatched diagnostic in the same code path.\n\n**THE CLAUSE-(d) MUTANT:** change the new `return;` to `break;`, keeping the latch and the\nannouncement.  Execution resumes at the memory write and consumes the uninitialised buffer -- **it\nbreaks the no-write contract without restoring `random()`**.  `rgb samemode` and `rgb tiled64` both\nexpected to stay green (supported RGB/RGBA, never reaching the default).  *The coverage gap is wider\nthan MTE: the scoped scripts never select an unsupported destination colour type at all, so their\ngreen results cannot establish this decline.*\n\n*** THE WITNESS PROBLEM IS CHARACTERISED, AND IT IS HARDER THAN THE ROW ASSUMED.  From the codex\nORACLE answer (`regress/artifacts/sgirandomcolour/codex_oracle_20260907.txt`), read in full\n2026-09-07 -- most of this had been sitting unread. ***\n\n**TWO PREMISE CORRECTIONS FIRST, and the second changes the FIX, not just the witness:**\n\n1. *\"The buffer lacks a declaration initializer; it is not NEVER INITIALIZED.  PUT arms assign\n   prefixes, and GET fills the requested transfer length.\"*  The row's shorthand was sloppy.\n2. *** \"THE MISSING `break` IS NOT WHAT PERMITS THE WRITE.  The write is AFTER the switch, so adding\n   `break` would still reach it.  **The proposed return matters.**\" ***  A `break`-shaped repair\n   would look right and do nothing -- exactly the sketch-versus-enclosing-control-flow rule this\n   project minted after four readers proposed a `return` into a function with a common tail.\n\n*** THE OBSERVER EXISTS, AND IT IS DISQUALIFIED BY THE PROBE'S OWN EXECUTION MODE. ***\n\nThe fork has debugger WRITE-WATCHPOINTS, and the common memory path checks them **before**\ndevice/cache/RAM dispatch -- *\"neither `PHYSICAL` nor `NO_EXCEPTIONS` excludes this helper's\nwrite\"* (`memory_rw.c:273`).  A matching attempt prints `WATCHPOINT: write`, which a pty driver can\ncount with no instrumented build.  **But two conditions kill it as written:**\n\n    memory_rw.c:273   the check requires  !single_step\n                      -- and sgi_re_randompixel_probe.py's run_ops() uses `step'.  Adding\n                      watchpoints to that scheme \"could report ZERO DESPITE WRITES.\"\n\n    same check        also requires  !about_to_enter_single_step, and SETS that flag after its\n                      first report -- so subsequent pixel writes inside the SAME drawing command\n                      go unreported.\n\n> **This is the carrier's single-stepping rule arriving on a new mechanism.**  It already records\n> that a stepped probe cannot observe interrupt DELIVERY (`cpu_dyntrans.c`: *\"Do not cause\n> interrupts while single-stepping\"*).  **Watchpoints are a second capability gated the same way**,\n> and the same discriminator applies: ask what the observation depends on before designing around\n> it.\n\n**WHAT THE MECHANISM CAN AND CANNOT SUPPORT, in codex's words:** it supports an\n**any-attempt-to-this-range** witness *\"when observation is active throughout the relevant\nexecution\"* -- i.e. FREE-RUNNING, not stepped -- and even then only the first report survives the\nflag.  It *\"cannot automatically support 'exactly N calls', or 'no writes anywhere from this\nhelper'.  A literal, attributed call count needs a suitable host-side observer or `harness:`\nwitness.\"*\n\n*** AND Q3 DEMOLISHES THE CHEAP ORACLE: \"unchanged destination bytes prove only unchanged OBSERVED\nCONTENTS.\" ***  Equality at the observation points is also consistent with a same-value write, a\nwrite followed by restoration, a write to somewhere else, no drawing operation at all, or an earlier\nhelper return.  **Two of those are live here rather than theoretical**: the helper returns on an\ninvalid tile pointer (`dev_sgi_re.c:274`), and *\"the memory path can discard an out-of-RAM write\nwhile returning success; corresponding reads can return zeros\"* (`memory_rw.c:662`).\n\n> **So a destination-equality row needs a control establishing that the pre-paint REALLY EXISTS and\n> that a SUPPORTED operation through the same path actually lands** -- otherwise \"unchanged\" is\n> indistinguishable from \"nothing ever happened\", which is the vacuity shape in its purest form.\n\n**THE TWO VIABLE PATHS, neither cheap:**\n* **(a) a FREE-RUNNING probe with a write-watchpoint** -- any-attempt semantics, first report only,\n  and it must abandon the stepped `run_ops()` idiom the sibling probes use;\n* **(b) a `harness:` witness with a host-side observer** -- an attributed call count, which the\n  ladder admits only when the round's subject IS the apparatus, so it would need care not to become\n  the rung-1 shortcut the ladder exists to refuse.\n\n**STATUS: this row is NOT held for fable5 and is the only HIGH row that is not** -- but its blocker\nis now a characterised capability gap rather than an open question, and neither path is a session's\nwork.  *Recorded in full because most of this analysis had been sitting unread in the artifact since\nit was produced, which is this session's most-repeated failure.*\n\n*** DESIGN PASS 1, 2026-09-08.  FIVE SEATS, UNANIMOUS ON BOTH QUESTIONS -- and the strongest answer\nREFUSED TO CERTIFY ITSELF, which is the part worth keeping. ***\n\n**THE DISPOSITION: member 3, DECLINE + LATCHED ANNOUNCE, implemented as candidate (b) -- skip the\nPUT's `memory_rw` for a declined mode.  Never an early `return`.**\n\n*** grok's REFRAMING IS BETTER THAN MY OPTION LIST AND IT DISSOLVES THE QUESTION THE ROW WAS FILED\nON. ***  The row asked *\"what should a declined BUFFER FILL leave behind?\"*, treating `buf` as the\nanalogue of `#504`'s `*color`.  It is not:\n\n> *\"Destination of a PUT is guest memory at `tileptr + ofs`, not `buf`.  `#504` declined the\n> helper's OUTPUT.  The PUT output is the store.  Skipping the store leaves whatever the guest\n> already had -- deterministic, no invented colour, no host bytes.  `buf` can stay uninitialised\n> because it is not sent.\"*\n\nSo the fix is not a fill policy at all.  **The question \"what should the buffer contain\" was the\nwrong question**, and my option (a) (zero-fill) was demoted to a fallback for exactly one case:\nif some tail consumes `buf` or assumes the store happened.\n\n**AND IT CORRECTED MY (a) IN PASSING:** zero-filling only `buf[0..3]` to mirror the `random()` shape\nis NOT (a) -- `bufdepth` can be 8, so that form *\"is this arm already instantiating\n`sgirebufuninit`\"*. I\nwill not guess `x++`, a dirty bit, a return value, or a use of `buf`.  I cannot certify (b) against\n'nine lines down.'\"*  That is this project's own sketch rule, applied by a seat to its own\nrecommendation, unprompted beyond the caution in the brief.\n\n## *** BOTH OF ITS UNCHECKABLE PREMISES ARE NOW SETTLED BY ME, BY OPENING THE FILE ***\n\n**1. THERE IS NO COMMON TAIL.**  `horrible_getputpixel()` is `void`, and after the PUT store the\n`else` runs the GET path and the function ENDS -- `}` closing the else, `}` closing the function,\nnothing between them.  No dirty bit, no counter, no return value, no later use of `buf`.\n**So (b) is safe**, and the `put_ok` flag form grok gave is belt-and-braces rather than necessary --\nwhich is still the right way to write it, because it cannot become the early-`return` trap under a\nlater edit.\n\n**2. `fatal()` DOES NOT EXIT.**  `src/core/debugmsg.c`: `va_start; va_debug; va_end;` and return --\nno `exit()`, no early-out.  grok flagged that if `fatal()` aborted, *\"the random fill is dead and\nthe guest-visible row collapses.\"*  It does not abort, so **the random bytes really do reach\n`memory_rw(..., MEM_WRITE)` and the defect stands.**\n\n*That premise should have been IN the packet. Reading all five:\n\n## THE DETECTOR -- convergent, and it is a DESTINATION CANARY, not \"looks non-random\"\n\nPre-fill the target bytes with a distinctive non-zero canary, execute ONE PUT with a declined mode,\nread back, and **pass iff the canary is bit-identical** -- i.e. the oracle is *the store did not\nhappen*, not *the value is not random*.  A liveness control in the SAME script repeats with a\ndefined mode (RGBA or CI) and the canary MUST change; without it a dead PUT path greens the round.\n\n*** THE HOLE, and three of the five seats were in it: DO NOT PIN THE TEST TO `0xf0` ALONE. ***\ndeepseek and kimi both proposed YCRCB `0xf0` as *the* test mode, kimi arguing it is the value a real\nguest would plausibly select.  grok:\n\n> *\"A mutant `case 0xf0: put_ok = 0; break;` with `default:` still random would escape.  Use at\n> least one UNDOCUMENTED value (`0x40` is the first hole) as the primary; `0xf0` can be a second\n> vector, not the only one.\"*\n\n**That is a real escape against the majority design.**  The documented mode is the most tempting\nchoice and the weakest one, because a mutant can special-case exactly it.\n\n**FOUR MORE STRENGTHENINGS, one per seat, none of them duplicated:**\n* **grok** -- vary `bufdepth` over at least {1, 4, 8}, so a mutant that skips the store only when\n  `bufdepth == 4` dies.\n* **minimax** -- make the oracle **CONJUNCTIVE**: the latch transitioned 0 -> 1 *during that pixel*\n  (record the PRE-state) AND `memory_rw` was not called for it.  Either conjunct alone is escapable,\n  and recording the pre-state defeats a mutant that pre-sets the latch at init.\n* **kimi** -- assert the announce count is **exactly 1**, which is what actually pins the latch.\n* **glm** -- assert the ADJACENT bytes are unchanged too, since the write is only `bufdepth` bytes\n  at `ofs`; and run twice asserting identical transcripts.\n\n**AND grok'S HONEST LIMIT ON ITS OWN ORACLE:** keeping `buf[i] = random()` while setting\n`put_ok = 0` leaves the canary intact, so a destination-only oracle cannot see the leftover\n`random()` calls.
